## 問題 52（#1204）英語／語彙／空所に入る語

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:05／管理番号：#1204
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 問題文

次の英文の空所（　　）に入る最も適切な語を選べ。

The results of the experiment (　　　　) greatly depending on the temperature.

### 選択肢

A. suffer （誤答：文脈に合わない別の語）
B. confirm （誤答：文脈に合わない別の語）
C. vary （正解）
D. generate （誤答：文脈に合わない別の語）

### 正解

vary

### ヒント

「温度によって」という条件が、結果がどのようになるかを説明している点に注目しよう。

### 解説

訳: その実験の結果は、温度によって大きく異なる。

「温度によって大きく」結果が変わるという文脈なので、「結果が異なる」という意味の動詞が入る。generate（生み出す）、suffer（苦しむ）、confirm（確かめる）は文法上は動詞として置けるが、「結果が温度によって異なる」という意味にはならない。

答え: vary（異なる）

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 例文の点検：例文に「vary」がちょうど 1 回、ほかの選択肢の語は入っていない、訳・解説は日本語
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（3 つの確認）
  - ○ 正解が合うか：正解「vary」は通る（vary は「変化する・ばらつく」の意味で、「温度によって結果が大きく変わる」という自然な表現。）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかの選択肢は合わない
  - ○ 正解を抜いた判定：ほかの選択肢はどれも合わない
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（3 つの確認）
  - ○ 正解が合うか：正解「vary」は通る（"vary" は「変化する・ばらつく」の意味で、結果が温度によって大きく変わるという内容に自然に合う。）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかの選択肢は合わない
  - ○ 正解を抜いた判定：ほかの選択肢はどれも合わない

電池

スマートフォンの電源を維持するために欠かせないものとして、電池が文脈に最も適しています。電池が切れることを示す「dying」という表現からも、電力を供給するものが求められていることがわかります。

## 問題 56（#1195）英語／読解／物語・体験談

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:04／管理番号：#1195
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

A Letter Across the Ocean

When my English teacher, Ms. Harper, announced that our class would have an international pen pal project, I was not excited at all. I was a shy first-year student, and the idea of writing to a stranger in another country made my stomach feel tight. Still, I signed up, mostly because my best friend, Yuki, had already chosen to join.

A few weeks later, I received a letter from a girl named Amara who lived in Ghana. Her handwriting was neat and careful, and she wrote about her school, her younger brother, and her love of football. She asked me what kinds of music I listened to and whether it snowed in my hometown. I read the letter three times that evening, smiling at her jokes.

At first, my replies were short and stiff. I worried that my English was too simple and that she would find me boring. Amara never seemed to mind. Her next letter was even longer, and she included a small photo of herself holding a red ball. I decided that I had to write something more honest, so I told her about my fear of speaking in front of people and my secret plan to become a teacher someday.

Months passed, and our letters grew more personal. When I finally wrote about my grandmother's illness, Amara replied with a message that made me cry. She told me that her own grandfather had been sick the year before, and that writing down her feelings had helped her get through it.

At the end of the project, our class held a small celebration. I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal. My voice shook at the beginning, but I kept going. When I looked up, I saw Ms. Harper nodding with a warm smile. I realized that the girl I had almost refused to write to had taught me more about courage than any lesson in class.

### 問題文

What did the writer do at the class celebration held at the end of the project?

### 選択肢

A. The writer read a short speech about their pen pal. （正解）
B. The writer sent Amara a photo of a red ball. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
C. The writer read a short speech in front of an empty classroom. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. The writer shared Amara's letters with the whole class. （誤答：本文に書かれていない）

### 正解

The writer read a short speech about their pen pal.

### ヒント

最終段落の前半を読みましょう。プロジェクトの終わりに行われた、クラスでの祝いの会での語り手の行動が書かれています。

### 解説

正解は、文通相手についての短いスピーチを読んだという内容です。本文には「I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal.」（私は立ち上がって、文通相手についての短いスピーチを読んだ）とあります。語り手は祝いの会で、文通相手についてのスピーチをしたことが分かります。赤いボールの写真を送ったのはアマラであり、語り手ではないので誤りです。祝いの会は空の教室ではなく、本文ではハーパー先生が聞いて微笑んでいるので、空の教室で読んだという内容は本文と食い違います。アマラの手紙をクラス全体で共有したという記述はありません。

本文の根拠: “I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 本文の点検：本文 324 語・英文に日本語がない
- ○ 本文の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 本文に誤りは見つからなかった
- × 設問の点検：設問 1: 誤答「Because Ms. Harper had asked the writer's family to help.」の種類が not_stated だが、中心の語が本文にある（本文と食い違うなら contradicts、一部だけ違うなら partial、細部の違いなら detail に直す）／設問 1: 解説が選択肢を A・B などの記号で呼んでいる（選択肢は並べ替えるので中身で呼ぶ）／設問 2: 解説が選択肢を A・B などの記号で呼んでいる（選択肢は並べ替えるので中身で呼ぶ）／設問 3: 誤答「The writer wrote a letter to Ms. Harper
- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 329 語）
- ○ 本文なしの確認（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：本文を見せずに解かせた答えは X（本文がないと分からない）
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（2 つの確認）
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「The writer read a short speech about their pen pal.」は裏付けあり（引用: "I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal." — 本文どおり、短いスピーチを読んだと書かれている。）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（2 つの確認）
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「The writer read a short speech about their pen pal.」は裏付けあり（引用: "I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal." — 本文どおり、設問の答えに当たる。）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない

## 問題 57（#1194）英語／読解／物語・体験談

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:04／管理番号：#1194
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

A Letter Across the Ocean

When my English teacher, Ms. Harper, announced that our class would have an international pen pal project, I was not excited at all. I was a shy first-year student, and the idea of writing to a stranger in another country made my stomach feel tight. Still, I signed up, mostly because my best friend, Yuki, had already chosen to join.

A few weeks later, I received a letter from a girl named Amara who lived in Ghana. Her handwriting was neat and careful, and she wrote about her school, her younger brother, and her love of football. She asked me what kinds of music I listened to and whether it snowed in my hometown. I read the letter three times that evening, smiling at her jokes.

At first, my replies were short and stiff. I worried that my English was too simple and that she would find me boring. Amara never seemed to mind. Her next letter was even longer, and she included a small photo of herself holding a red ball. I decided that I had to write something more honest, so I told her about my fear of speaking in front of people and my secret plan to become a teacher someday.

Months passed, and our letters grew more personal. When I finally wrote about my grandmother's illness, Amara replied with a message that made me cry. She told me that her own grandfather had been sick the year before, and that writing down her feelings had helped her get through it.

At the end of the project, our class held a small celebration. I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal. My voice shook at the beginning, but I kept going. When I looked up, I saw Ms. Harper nodding with a warm smile. I realized that the girl I had almost refused to write to had taught me more about courage than any lesson in class.

### 問題文

What did the writer say they learned from Amara?

### 選択肢

A. That writing letters to strangers was a waste of time. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
B. That Amara had taught the writer more about bravery than any class lesson. （正解）
C. That a classmate could teach the writer a new sport. （誤答：本文に書かれていない）
D. That Amara had visited the writer's class to give a speech about courage. （誤答：本文と食い違う／見直しでは「本文に書かれていない」）

### 正解

That Amara had taught the writer more about bravery than any class lesson.

### ヒント

最終段落の終わりの部分を読みましょう。語り手がアマラから得たものについての気づきが書かれています。

### 解説

正解は、アマラが授業のどの教訓よりも勇気について多くを教えてくれたという内容です。本文には「the girl I had almost refused to write to had taught me more about courage than any lesson in class」（ほとんど手紙を書くのを断りそうになった相手が、授業のどの教訓よりも勇気について自分に教えてくれた）とあります。つまり、アマラは授業よりも勇気について多くを教えてくれた、ということです。クラスメイトから運動を教わったという記述はないので誤りです。手紙のやり取りが無駄だったという考えは本文と逆で、語り手は手紙を通じて多くを学んでいるので誤りです。アマラが語り手のクラスを訪れて講演したという記述はなく、本文と食い違います。

本文の根拠: “I realized that the girl I had almost refused to write to had taught me more about courage than any lesson in class.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「That Amara had taught the writer more about bravery than any」は裏付けあり（引用: "I realized that the girl I had almost refused to write to had taught me more about courage than）

## 問題 58（#1193）英語／読解／物語・体験談

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:04／管理番号：#1193
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

A Letter Across the Ocean

When my English teacher, Ms. Harper, announced that our class would have an international pen pal project, I was not excited at all. I was a shy first-year student, and the idea of writing to a stranger in another country made my stomach feel tight. Still, I signed up, mostly because my best friend, Yuki, had already chosen to join.

A few weeks later, I received a letter from a girl named Amara who lived in Ghana. Her handwriting was neat and careful, and she wrote about her school, her younger brother, and her love of football. She asked me what kinds of music I listened to and whether it snowed in my hometown. I read the letter three times that evening, smiling at her jokes.

At first, my replies were short and stiff. I worried that my English was too simple and that she would find me boring. Amara never seemed to mind. Her next letter was even longer, and she included a small photo of herself holding a red ball. I decided that I had to write something more honest, so I told her about my fear of speaking in front of people and my secret plan to become a teacher someday.

Months passed, and our letters grew more personal. When I finally wrote about my grandmother's illness, Amara replied with a message that made me cry. She told me that her own grandfather had been sick the year before, and that writing down her feelings had helped her get through it.

At the end of the project, our class held a small celebration. I stood up and read a short speech about my pen pal. My voice shook at the beginning, but I kept going. When I looked up, I saw Ms. Harper nodding with a warm smile. I realized that the girl I had almost refused to write to had taught me more about courage than any lesson in class.

### 問題文

Why did the writer first decide to join the pen pal project?

### 選択肢

A. Because the writer's best friend, Yuki, had already decided to join. （正解）
B. Because the writer wanted to make new friends in Ghana. （誤答：本文に書かれていない／見直しでは「本文と食い違う」）
C. Because Ms. Harper had personally asked the writer to join the project. （誤答：本文と食い違う／見直しでは「本文に書かれていない」）
D. Because the writer was excited about writing to a stranger abroad. （誤答：本文と食い違う）

### 正解

Because the writer's best friend, Yuki, had already decided to join.

### ヒント

第1段落を読みましょう。語り手がプロジェクトに申し込んだ理由が書かれています。

### 解説

正解は、親友のユキがすでに参加を決めていたからだという内容です。本文には「I signed up, mostly because my best friend, Yuki, had already chosen to join.」（私が申し込んだのは、主に親友のユキがすでに参加を決めていたからだ）とあります。語り手が申し込んだ主な理由は、親友が先に参加していたことだと分かります。ガーナの友達を作りたかったという内容は書かれていないので誤りです。手紙を書くことに乗り気だったという内容は、本文の「I was not excited at all.」（まったく乗り気ではなかった）と食い違うので誤りです。ハーパー先生が語り手に個人的に頼んだという内容も、先生はクラス全体にプロジェクトを知らせただけなので、本文と食い違います。

本文の根拠: “I signed up, mostly because my best friend, Yuki, had already chosen to join.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 329 語）

  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない

## 問題 59（#1188）英語／読解／物語・体験談

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:03／管理番号：#1188
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

The Dog Who Came Home

My family moved to a small town near the coast in the spring of my second year of high school. I had not wanted to leave Osaka, and for weeks I kept to myself. The only living thing that seemed to care about my new life was Kuro, our black-and-white mixed-breed dog, who followed me to the bus stop every morning.

On a cold Sunday in November, Kuro slipped out the back gate while my father was carrying groceries inside. We did not notice until it was nearly dark. My father drove around the neighborhood, and my mother called the animal shelter. I went out on my bicycle, calling his name until my voice was hoarse. Nobody answered except the wind.

By eight o'clock, I was sitting on the porch steps with my phone in my hand, wondering whether I would ever see him again. I felt guilty. I had been the one who was supposed to check the gate that morning, and I had forgotten. Tears came before I could stop them.

Then my phone buzzed. It was a message from Mrs. Nakamura, an elderly woman who lived two streets away. "I think I have your dog," she wrote. "He is sitting outside my door and will not move."

I ran the whole way there. When I turned the corner, I saw Kuro sitting calmly beside a flower pot, his tail sweeping the ground. He looked up at me as if nothing had happened. I knelt down, held him tightly, and laughed through my tears.

Mrs. Nakamura smiled and told me she had found him shivering near the station. She had given him a warm blanket and some leftover fish, and he had decided that her porch was a good place to wait. "He knew where he belonged," she said.

Walking home with Kuro pulling happily at his leash, I realized that I had also started to feel at home here. The town was not so unfriendly after all.

### 問題文

How did the writer's feelings about the town change by the end of the story?

### 選択肢

A. The writer became close friends with Mrs. Nakamura. （誤答：本文に書かれていない）
B. The writer came to feel at home in the town. （正解）
C. The writer felt at home, but only at Mrs. Nakamura's house. （誤答：一部だけ合っている／見直しでは「本文と食い違う」）
D. The writer decided to move back to Osaka. （誤答：本文と食い違う）

### 正解

The writer came to feel at home in the town.

### ヒント

最後の段落を読み、語り手が町についてどう感じるようになったかを考えましょう。

### 解説

正解は「語り手は町に居心地のよさを感じるようになった」という内容です。根拠の文では、Kuroと家に帰る途中で、語り手もこの町で居心地よく感じ始めていると気づいたと書かれています。ほかの選択肢のうち、大阪に戻ることを決めたという記述はなく、本文と食い違います。Mrs. Nakamuraと親しい友人になったという記述はありません。また、町に居心地のよさを感じたのはMrs. Nakamuraの家にいたときではなく、家へ帰る途中であり、一部だけ合っている誤りです。

本文の根拠: “Walking home with Kuro pulling happily at his leash, I realized that I had also started to feel at home here.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 本文の点検：本文 330 語・英文に日本語がない
- ○ 本文の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 本文に誤りは見つからなかった
- × 設問の点検：設問 1: 誤答「At the animal shelter, where he had been taken」の種類が not_stated だが、中心の語が本文にある（本文と食い違うなら contradicts、一部だけ違うなら partial、細部の違いなら detail に直す）／設問 1: ヒントの「第4段落」が、根拠の文のある段落（第5段落）と違う（直しを依頼します）
- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 335 語）
- ○ 本文なしの確認（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：本文を見せずに解かせた答えは X（本文がないと分からない）

  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「The writer came to feel at home in the town.」は裏付けあり（引用: "Walking home with Kuro pulling happily at his leash, I realized that I had also started to feel）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない
  - ○ 誤答の種類：判定が分かれた（そのまま）：「The writer felt at home, but only at Mrs」作成役 一部だけ合っている／見直し 本文と食い違う
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（2 つの確認）

## 問題 60（#1186）英語／読解／物語・体験談

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:03／管理番号：#1186
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

The Dog Who Came Home

My family moved to a small town near the coast in the spring of my second year of high school. I had not wanted to leave Osaka, and for weeks I kept to myself. The only living thing that seemed to care about my new life was Kuro, our black-and-white mixed-breed dog, who followed me to the bus stop every morning.

On a cold Sunday in November, Kuro slipped out the back gate while my father was carrying groceries inside. We did not notice until it was nearly dark. My father drove around the neighborhood, and my mother called the animal shelter. I went out on my bicycle, calling his name until my voice was hoarse. Nobody answered except the wind.

By eight o'clock, I was sitting on the porch steps with my phone in my hand, wondering whether I would ever see him again. I felt guilty. I had been the one who was supposed to check the gate that morning, and I had forgotten. Tears came before I could stop them.

Then my phone buzzed. It was a message from Mrs. Nakamura, an elderly woman who lived two streets away. "I think I have your dog," she wrote. "He is sitting outside my door and will not move."

I ran the whole way there. When I turned the corner, I saw Kuro sitting calmly beside a flower pot, his tail sweeping the ground. He looked up at me as if nothing had happened. I knelt down, held him tightly, and laughed through my tears.

Mrs. Nakamura smiled and told me she had found him shivering near the station. She had given him a warm blanket and some leftover fish, and he had decided that her porch was a good place to wait. "He knew where he belonged," she said.

Walking home with Kuro pulling happily at his leash, I realized that I had also started to feel at home here. The town was not so unfriendly after all.

### 問題文

Where did the writer finally find Kuro?

### 選択肢

A. On the porch of the writer's own house （誤答：本文と食い違う）
B. At the animal shelter, where he had been taken （誤答：本文と食い違う）
C. Beside a flower pot at an elderly neighbor's house （正解）
D. At the station, where Mrs. Nakamura had first seen him （誤答：一部だけ合っている／見直しでは「本文と食い違う」）

### 正解

Beside a flower pot at an elderly neighbor's house

### ヒント

第5段落で、語り手がKuroをみつけたときのKuroのようすと場所に注目しましょう。

### 解説

正解は「近所のお年寄りの家の、植木鉢のそばに座っているKuroを見つけた」という内容です。根拠の文では、語り手が角を曲がると、Kuroが植木鉢のそばで落ち着いて座っており、しっぽで地面をはいていたと述べられています。ほかの選択肢のうち、動物保護施設にKuroが連れて行かれたという記述は本文と食い違います。母が保護施設に電話したのは行方を探すためで、Kuroが施設にいたのではありません。語り手の家のポーチで見つかったという記述もなく、本文と食い違います。駅でKuroが見つかったのはMrs. Nakamuraが震えているKuroを見つけた場所であり、語り手が見つけた場所ではないので、一部だけ合っている誤りです。

本文の根拠: “When I turned the corner, I saw Kuro sitting calmly beside a flower pot, his tail sweeping the ground.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

## 問題 62（#1184）英語／読解／説明文

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:01／管理番号：#1184
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Why Reading Books Still Matters

In an age of smartphones and instant video, many people wonder whether reading books is still worth the time. The answer is yes. Reading offers benefits that are hard to get from other activities, and these benefits affect the mind, the way we communicate, and even our health.

The first benefit is the growth of vocabulary and understanding. When readers meet unfamiliar words in a story or an essay, they often work out their meaning from the context. Over time, this process builds a larger vocabulary. Studies of language learning have repeatedly shown that people who read regularly tend to understand complex sentences more easily than those who mostly read short messages. Books also train readers to follow long arguments, a skill that is useful in school and at work.

Second, reading improves the ability to imagine other points of view. A novel asks readers to live inside the thoughts of someone whose life is very different from their own. For example, a student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new country may begin to understand the feelings of newcomers in her own town. Psychologists call this ability empathy, and it helps people get along with others in daily life.

Third, reading can give the mind a rest. Unlike many screens, a book does not send constant alerts or advertisements. Readers can choose their own pace and stop when they want. Many people say that even twenty minutes of reading before bed helps them relax and sleep better. In this way, a book can be a quiet place in a busy day.

In conclusion, books do much more than entertain. They build language skills, encourage understanding of other people, and offer a calm break from the noise of modern life. For these reasons, reading books is a habit worth keeping, even in a world full of new kinds of media.

### 問題文

According to the article, what example does the writer give to show that reading helps people feel empathy?

### 選択肢

A. A student who writes a diary about her summer holiday learns to draw maps of new towns. （誤答：本文に書かれていない）
B. A student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new country begins to understand the feelings of newcomers in her own town. （正解）
C. A student who reads only short messages on her phone becomes more skilled at following long arguments. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. A student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new city begins to understand newcomers in her own town. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え）

### 正解

A student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new country begins to understand the feelings of newcomers in her own town.

### ヒント

第3段落で、物語を読むことによって別の人の立場を想像できるようになる例を探してみましょう。

### 解説

正解は「新しい国に引っ越した家族の物語を読んだ生徒が、自分の町に来た新住民の気持ちを理解し始める」という内容です。第3段落では、新しい国に移ってきた家族の話を読んだ生徒が、町の新しい住民の気持ちを理解するようになる例が挙げられています。「新しい都市」に移った家族の話になっている選択肢は、本文の「新しい国」と違うので誤りです。夏休みの日記や地図の話は本文に出てきません。「短いメッセージばかり読む生徒が長い議論を追う力を身につける」という内容は、本文で長い議論を追う力は読書によって身につくと書かれているので、本文と食い違います。

本文の根拠: “For example, a student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new country may begin to understand the feelings of newcomers in her own town.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

## 問題 63（#1183）英語／読解／説明文

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 20:01／管理番号：#1183
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Why Reading Books Still Matters

In an age of smartphones and instant video, many people wonder whether reading books is still worth the time. The answer is yes. Reading offers benefits that are hard to get from other activities, and these benefits affect the mind, the way we communicate, and even our health.

The first benefit is the growth of vocabulary and understanding. When readers meet unfamiliar words in a story or an essay, they often work out their meaning from the context. Over time, this process builds a larger vocabulary. Studies of language learning have repeatedly shown that people who read regularly tend to understand complex sentences more easily than those who mostly read short messages. Books also train readers to follow long arguments, a skill that is useful in school and at work.

Second, reading improves the ability to imagine other points of view. A novel asks readers to live inside the thoughts of someone whose life is very different from their own. For example, a student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new country may begin to understand the feelings of newcomers in her own town. Psychologists call this ability empathy, and it helps people get along with others in daily life.

Third, reading can give the mind a rest. Unlike many screens, a book does not send constant alerts or advertisements. Readers can choose their own pace and stop when they want. Many people say that even twenty minutes of reading before bed helps them relax and sleep better. In this way, a book can be a quiet place in a busy day.

In conclusion, books do much more than entertain. They build language skills, encourage understanding of other people, and offer a calm break from the noise of modern life. For these reasons, reading books is a habit worth keeping, even in a world full of new kinds of media.

### 問題文

According to the article, why can reading give the mind a rest?

### 選択肢

A. Readers can finish a book quickly, so they never feel rushed. （誤答：本文に書かれていない）
B. Books do not keep interrupting readers with notices and promotions. （正解）
C. Books let readers choose their pace, so they never need to stop reading. （誤答：本文と食い違う／見直しでは「一部だけ合っている」）
D. Books contain more advertisements than most screens do. （誤答：本文と食い違う）

### 正解

Books do not keep interrupting readers with notices and promotions.

### ヒント

第4段落を読み、本とスクリーンを比べている部分に注目しましょう。

### 解説

正解は「本は通知や広告で読者の邪魔をし続けない」という内容です。第4段落には「多くのスクリーンと違い、本は絶え間ない通知や広告を送ってこない」と書かれており、これが心を休ませる理由として挙げられています。「本はすぐに読み終えられるので急がされない」という内容は、本文に書かれていません。「本のほうがスクリーンより広告が多い」という内容は、本文と反対で、スクリーンの方が通知や広告が多いと書かれています。「読むペースは選べるが、いつでも読むのをやめられないので読み続けなければならない」という内容は、本文で読者は好きなときに止められると書かれているので、本文と食い違います。

本文の根拠: “Unlike many screens, a book does not send constant alerts or advertisements.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Why Reading Books Still Matters

In an age of smartphones and instant video, many people wonder whether reading books is still worth the time. The answer is yes. Reading offers benefits that are hard to get from other activities, and these benefits affect the mind, the way we communicate, and even our health.

The first benefit is the growth of vocabulary and understanding. When readers meet unfamiliar words in a story or an essay, they often work out their meaning from the context. Over time, this process builds a larger vocabulary. Studies of language learning have repeatedly shown that people who read regularly tend to understand complex sentences more easily than those who mostly read short messages. Books also train readers to follow long arguments, a skill that is useful in school and at work.

Second, reading improves the ability to imagine other points of view. A novel asks readers to live inside the thoughts of someone whose life is very different from their own. For example, a student who reads a story about a family that has moved to a new country may begin to understand the feelings of newcomers in her own town. Psychologists call this ability empathy, and it helps people get along with others in daily life.

Third, reading can give the mind a rest. Unlike many screens, a book does not send constant alerts or advertisements. Readers can choose their own pace and stop when they want. Many people say that even twenty minutes of reading before bed helps them relax and sleep better. In this way, a book can be a quiet place in a busy day.

In conclusion, books do much more than entertain. They build language skills, encourage understanding of other people, and offer a calm break from the noise of modern life. For these reasons, reading books is a habit worth keeping, even in a world full of new kinds of media.

### 問題文

What is the main purpose of the passage?

### 選択肢

A. To argue that reading books remains valuable in the modern world. （正解）
B. To show that reading books is useful only for students at school. （誤答：一部だけ合っている／見直しでは「本文と食い違う」）
C. To suggest that new media have made reading books unnecessary. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. To explain how to choose the best books for young children. （誤答：本文に書かれていない）

### 正解

To argue that reading books remains valuable in the modern world.

### ヒント

第1段落と最後の段落を読み、筆者が全体を通して何を主張しているかを考えてみましょう。

### 解説

正解は「現代でも読書には価値があると主張している」という内容です。筆者は第1段落で「その答えはイエスだ」と述べ、読書は心、コミュニケーションの仕方、そして健康にまで影響する利点があると説明しています。最後の段落でも、新しいメディアがあふれる世界でも読書は続ける価値があると結論づけています。「学校の生徒にしか役立たない」という内容は、本文が仕事でも役立つと述べているので一部しか合っていません。「幼い子ども向けの本の選び方」については本文に書かれていません。「新しいメディアが読書を不要にした」という考えは、本文の主張と逆です。

本文の根拠: “Reading offers benefits that are hard to get from other activities, and these benefits affect the mind, the way we communicate, and even our health.”

## 問題 67（#1170）英語／読解／説明文

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:58／管理番号：#1170
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Why Learning a Second Language Still Matters

Many people assume that learning a second language is useful only for those who plan to become interpreters or who will move abroad. In reality, the benefits reach far beyond such special cases. Learning another language changes how the brain works, improves everyday communication, and opens doors to new cultures and opportunities.

One of the most interesting benefits is cognitive. When people use two languages, they constantly choose which words to use and which to ignore. This regular mental exercise seems to strengthen the brain's ability to focus and switch between tasks. Researchers have suggested that bilingual people may be better at solving problems and at ignoring distractions, although the size of this effect is still debated among scientists.

Language learning also has practical value at work and in daily life. A worker who can speak with customers in their own language may build stronger relationships than one who relies only on a translation app. For example, a small restaurant owner named Hana, who learned Spanish during evening classes, found that her visitors from Mexico and Peru came back more often because they felt welcome. Such personal connections are difficult to measure, but they often matter a great deal.

Finally, learning a second language encourages understanding of other ways of thinking. Each language carries its own expressions, humor, and traditions, so students who study one often become more curious about the people who speak it. This curiosity can lead to friendships, travel, and a wider view of the world. In a global society, that kind of openness may be the most valuable benefit of all.

### 問題文

According to the article, what is the main idea of "Why Learning a Second Language Still Matters"?

### 選択肢

A. Learning a second language is necessary only for people who plan to become interpreters. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
B. Learning a second language mainly helps people who work with translation apps. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
C. Learning a second language has no effect on how people understand other cultures. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. Learning a second language is valuable for many people, not only for interpreters or people who move abroad. （正解）

### 正解

Learning a second language is valuable for many people, not only for interpreters or people who move abroad.

### ヒント

第1段落で、第二言語学習について多くの人が抱いている考えと、筆者の主張が示されています。両方を比べて読みましょう。

### 解説

正解は「第二言語の学習は、通訳になる人や海外に移る人だけでなく、多くの人にとって価値がある」という内容です。根拠の文は「実際には、その利益はそのような特別な場合をはるかに超えて広がる」という意味で、第1段落の主張と一致します。 「通訳になる人にだけ必要だ」という選択肢は、本文が特別な場合に限らないと述べているので誤りです。「翻訳アプリを使う人を主に助ける」は、本文が翻訳アプリだけに頼る人より、客と直接話せる人の方が関係を築けると述べているので誤りです。「文化の理解には影響しない」は、第4段落で文化への理解が深まると述べられているので誤りです。

本文の根拠: “In reality, the benefits reach far beyond such special cases.”

## 問題 68（#1165）英語／読解／説明文

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:57／管理番号：#1165
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Why Cities Get So Hot in Summer

Anyone who has walked through a city on a hot summer afternoon knows the feeling. Asphalt roads shimmer, concrete walls radiate warmth, and the air seems to press down on you. Scientists call this phenomenon the urban heat island effect. In simple terms, cities are often several degrees warmer than the rural areas around them, and the difference is most noticeable at night.

The main reason is the materials that cities are built from. Buildings, roads, and parking lots are made of concrete, asphalt, and brick. These materials absorb sunlight during the day and release the stored heat slowly after the sun goes down. In contrast, grass, trees, and soil absorb less heat and also cool the air through a process called evapotranspiration, in which plants release water vapor. Because cities have far fewer plants and less exposed soil, they lose this natural cooling.

Human activity adds to the problem. Air conditioners, cars, and factories all release heat into the air. Tall buildings can also make the situation worse. Their tightly packed shapes block the wind that might otherwise carry hot air away and bring in cooler air from the suburbs. As a result, heat can become trapped between buildings, especially on still summer nights.

The effects go beyond discomfort. Higher temperatures can increase electricity use, as people rely more on air conditioning, and they can put serious strain on the health of elderly people and young children. Fortunately, many cities are responding. Some are planting more trees, building green roofs, and painting roofs white to reflect sunlight. These simple changes show that the heat island effect is not inevitable, and that careful planning can make urban summers much more bearable.

### 問題文

According to the article, how do tall buildings make heat in cities worse?

### 選択肢

A. They bring hot air from the suburbs into the city. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
B. They block wind, and they also release heat from air conditioners. （誤答：一部だけ合っている）
C. They trap heat by blocking winds that could carry hot air away. （正解）
D. They let the wind carry hot air out, so the streets stay cool. （誤答：本文と食い違う）

### 正解

They trap heat by blocking winds that could carry hot air away.

### ヒント

第3段落の中ほどで、高い建物が風にどう影響するかを説明しています。風の役割に注目して読んでください。

### 解説

正解は「高い建物が、熱い空気を運び去るはずの風をさえぎることで、熱が建物の間にたまる」という内容です。根拠の文は「建物がぎっしり並んだ形は、熱い空気を運び去るはずの風をさえぎる」という意味です。「高い建物が郊外から熱い空気を都市に運び込む」という選択肢は、郊外からは涼しい空気が入ってくると本文にあるので、食い違います。「風が熱い空気を運び出して道路を涼しく保つ」という選択肢は、本文では高い建物が風をさえぎると述べられているので、食い違います。「高い建物は風をさえぎり、エアコンの熱も放出する」という選択肢は、風をさえぎることは正しいものの、エアコンの熱は本文で高い建物の働きとしては述べられておらず、一部だけ合っている内容です。

本文の根拠: “Their tightly packed shapes block the wind that might otherwise carry hot air away and bring in cooler air from the suburbs.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない
  - ○ 1 対 1 の判定：怪しい誤答 2 つは、どれも正解の方が成り立つ

## 問題 69（#1164）英語／読解／説明文

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:57／管理番号：#1164
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Why Cities Get So Hot in Summer

Anyone who has walked through a city on a hot summer afternoon knows the feeling. Asphalt roads shimmer, concrete walls radiate warmth, and the air seems to press down on you. Scientists call this phenomenon the urban heat island effect. In simple terms, cities are often several degrees warmer than the rural areas around them, and the difference is most noticeable at night.

The main reason is the materials that cities are built from. Buildings, roads, and parking lots are made of concrete, asphalt, and brick. These materials absorb sunlight during the day and release the stored heat slowly after the sun goes down. In contrast, grass, trees, and soil absorb less heat and also cool the air through a process called evapotranspiration, in which plants release water vapor. Because cities have far fewer plants and less exposed soil, they lose this natural cooling.

Human activity adds to the problem. Air conditioners, cars, and factories all release heat into the air. Tall buildings can also make the situation worse. Their tightly packed shapes block the wind that might otherwise carry hot air away and bring in cooler air from the suburbs. As a result, heat can become trapped between buildings, especially on still summer nights.

The effects go beyond discomfort. Higher temperatures can increase electricity use, as people rely more on air conditioning, and they can put serious strain on the health of elderly people and young children. Fortunately, many cities are responding. Some are planting more trees, building green roofs, and painting roofs white to reflect sunlight. These simple changes show that the heat island effect is not inevitable, and that careful planning can make urban summers much more bearable.

### 問題文

According to the article, what is the main reason cities get so hot in summer?

### 選択肢

A. Cities have fewer plants, so they lose the cooling that plants provide. （誤答：一部だけ合っている）
B. Cities have more plants than rural areas, so the air stays cooler. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
C. Building materials such as concrete and asphalt store heat during the day and release it slowly. （正解）
D. Strong winds blow hot air into cities from the countryside. （誤答：本文と食い違う）

### 正解

Building materials such as concrete and asphalt store heat during the day and release it slowly.

### ヒント

第2段落を読みましょう。都市をつくる材料が、熱をどのように扱うかに注目してください。

### 解説

正解は「コンクリートやアスファルトなどの建材が、昼間に熱をためて夜にゆっくり放出する」という内容です。根拠の文は「都市の暑さの主な理由は、都市をつくっている材料にある」という意味で、続く文では「これらの材料は昼間に日光を吸収し、日が沈んだ後にたまった熱をゆっくり放出する」と説明されています。「都市には農村より植物が多く、空気が涼しく保たれる」という選択肢は、本文が都市には植物がはるかに少ないと述べているので食い違います。「都市には植物が少なく、植物がもたらす冷却効果を失う」という選択肢は、植物が少ないことは本文にあるものの、それは理由の一部にすぎず、主な理由は材料だとされているので一部だけ正しい内容です。「強い風が郊外から熱い空気を都市に運ぶ」という選択肢は、本文では郊外からは涼しい空気が入ってくると書かれているので、食い違います。

本文の根拠: “The main reason is the materials that cities are built from.”

## 問題 70（#1162）英語／読解／説明文

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:56／管理番号：#1162
- レベル：共通テスト／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

From Sun Shade to Rain Shield: The History of the Umbrella

Today, many people carry an umbrella without thinking twice. However, the umbrella has a long history, and it was not first made to keep off rain. Its earliest purpose was to provide shade from the hot sun. Ancient artworks from Egypt and Assyria show important people being shaded by large, fan-like coverings held up by servants. Shade was a sign of status, so only the wealthy could enjoy it.

Over time, the umbrella spread to other parts of the world. In China, people made umbrellas from paper and bamboo, and some were coated with oil to make them waterproof. These designs were light and strong, and they influenced umbrellas in other countries. Trade routes carried these ideas westward, and by the medieval period, umbrellas were used in parts of Europe, although they were still often seen as a luxury item.

The umbrella became a common tool for rain in the eighteenth century. In London, a traveler named Jonas Hanway is often credited with helping to popularize it for use in wet weather. At that time, people usually relied on hats and heavy cloaks to stay dry. Because the umbrella was easy to carry and protected the whole body, it gradually changed from a curiosity into an everyday item for ordinary people.

Looking back, the umbrella's history shows how a simple object can change its purpose. What began as a symbol of power and a way to escape the heat became a practical tool that protects people from rain. Today, the umbrella is still designed to meet new needs, such as being lighter, more compact, and more durable.

### 問題文

Why did the umbrella gradually become an everyday item for ordinary people?

### 選択肢

A. Because it was easy to carry and the government ordered people to use it in wet weather. （誤答：一部だけ合っている／見直しでは「本文に書かれていない」）
B. Because it was easy to carry and cheaper to make than hats and heavy cloaks. （誤答：一部だけ合っている）
C. Because people had stopped wearing hats and heavy cloaks by then. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. Because it was easy to carry and covered the whole body. （正解）

### 正解

Because it was easy to carry and covered the whole body.

### ヒント

第3段落の後半に、傘が庶民の日常品になった理由を述べた文があります。

### 解説

正解は「傘は持ち運びやすく、体全体を守れたので、少しずつ珍しい物から庶民の日常品へと変わった」という内容です。第3段落には、「傘は持ち運びやすく、体全体を守れたので、少しずつ珍しい物から庶民の日常品へと変わっていった」と書かれています。「傘は持ち運びやすく、帽子や厚手のマントより安く作れた」という内容は、持ち運びやすい点は正しいものの、安く作れたという点は本文に書かれていないので誤りです。「傘は持ち運びやすく、政府が雨の日に使うよう命じた」という内容も、持ち運びやすい点は正しいものの、政府の命令については本文に書かれていないので誤りです。「人々が帽子や厚手のマントを身につけなくなった」という内容は、本文では当時の人々は帽子や厚手のマントに頼っていたと書かれているので誤りです。

本文の根拠: “Because the umbrella was easy to carry and protected the whole body, it gradually changed from a curiosity into an everyday item for ordinary people.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 本文の点検：本文 267 語・英文に日本語がない
- ○ 本文の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 本文に誤りは見つからなかった

## 問題 71（#1159）英語／読解／お知らせ・広告・メール

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:55／管理番号：#1159
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Notice: Changes to the Route 7 Bus Timetable

Starting Monday, April 6, the Route 7 bus will follow a new timetable. Please check the changes below before you travel.

Weekday buses will now leave Riverside Station at 7:15, 7:45, and 8:15 a.m. The 7:30 and 8:00 a.m. departures have been cancelled. On Saturdays, the first bus will leave at 8:30 a.m. instead of 9:00 a.m.

The fare for adult passengers remains $2.50, but passengers who pay with a Metro Card will now receive a 10% discount on every ride.

For more information, visit www.example.org/route7 or call the Riverside Transit Office at 555-0142.

### 問題文

What does the notice say about paying the fare with a Metro Card?

### 選択肢

A. Adult passengers who pay with a Metro Card now pay $2.75 per ride. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
B. Adult passengers who pay with a Metro Card get 10% off only their first ride. （誤答：本文と食い違う／見直しでは「一部だけ合っている」）
C. Passengers who pay with a Metro Card ride free after their first trip. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. Adult passengers who pay with a Metro Card get 10% off each ride. （正解）

### 正解

Adult passengers who pay with a Metro Card get 10% off each ride.

### ヒント

第3段落の運賃に関する部分を読みましょう。大人の運賃が変わったかどうかにも注意してください。

### 解説

正解は「Metro Cardで支払う乗客は、乗車のたびに10％の割引を受けられる」です。本文では「Metro Cardで支払う乗客は、どの乗車でも10％の割引を受けられる」と書かれています。大人の運賃は$2.50のままで変わりません。「割引は最初の乗車だけ」は、毎回の乗車に割引があるという本文と食い違います。「$2.75」は本文にない数字で、運賃は$2.50のままです。「最初の乗車の後は無料になる」も、割引は毎回の乗車に適用されるという本文と食い違います。

本文の根拠: “The fare for adult passengers remains $2.50, but passengers who pay with a Metro Card will now receive a 10% discount on every ride.”

### 本文

Notice: Changes to the Route 7 Bus Timetable

Starting Monday, April 6, the Route 7 bus will follow a new timetable. Please check the changes below before you travel.

Weekday buses will now leave Riverside Station at 7:15, 7:45, and 8:15 a.m. The 7:30 and 8:00 a.m. departures have been cancelled. On Saturdays, the first bus will leave at 8:30 a.m. instead of 9:00 a.m.

The fare for adult passengers remains $2.50, but passengers who pay with a Metro Card will now receive a 10% discount on every ride.

For more information, visit www.example.org/route7 or call the Riverside Transit Office at 555-0142.

### 問題文

According to the notice, what has changed for the first Saturday bus leaving Riverside Station?

### 選択肢

A. It now leaves 30 minutes later than before. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
B. It now leaves at 7:15 a.m. instead of 7:30 a.m. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え／見直しでは「本文に書かれていない」）
C. It has been cancelled from the Saturday timetable. （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. It now leaves 30 minutes earlier than before. （正解）

### 正解

It now leaves 30 minutes earlier than before.

### ヒント

第2段落にある、土曜日の始発バスの発車時刻の変更点を読みましょう。

### 解説

正解は「土曜日の始発は、以前より30分早く出発するようになる」です。本文では「土曜日は始発が午前9時ではなく午前8時30分に出発する」と書かれており、9時から8時30分へ30分早くなることが分かります。「30分遅くなる」は逆の内容で、「午前7時15分」は平日の始発の時刻です。「運休になる」という内容は本文にはなく、土曜日の始発は運行される時刻が変わるだけです。

本文の根拠: “On Saturdays, the first bus will leave at 8:30 a.m. instead of 9:00 a.m.”

## 問題 76（#1147）英語／読解／お知らせ・広告・メール

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:53／管理番号：#1147
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Notice of Changes to Library Opening Hours

Starting Monday, April 7, the Maple Hill Public Library will change its opening hours. The new schedule is designed to make the library easier to use for students and working adults.

From Monday to Friday, the library will open at 8:00 a.m. instead of 9:00 a.m., and it will close at 9:00 p.m. instead of 8:00 p.m. On Saturdays, the library will be open from 10:00 a.m. to 6:00 p.m. It will remain closed on Sundays.

Please note that the study rooms on the second floor will close 30 minutes before the library closes. Returned books can be placed in the outdoor drop box at any time.

For more information, visit www.example.org/library or call the front desk at 555-0142.

### 問題文

If the library closes at 9:00 p.m. on a weekday, what time will the second-floor study rooms close that day?

### 選択肢

A. 8:00 p.m. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え）
B. 9:00 p.m. （誤答：本文と食い違う／見直しでは「数・日時・人などの取り違え」）
C. 9:30 p.m. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え／見直しでは「本文と食い違う」）
D. 8:30 p.m. （正解）

### 正解

8:30 p.m.

### ヒント

第3段落に、2階の学習室の閉室時刻についての注意書きがあります。

### 解説

本文には「2階の学習室は図書館の閉館の30分前に閉まる」と書かれています（Please note that the study rooms on the second floor will close 30 minutes before the library closes.／2階の学習室は、図書館が閉まる30分前に閉まります）。平日の閉館時刻は新しく午後9時になるので（第2段落）、学習室はその30分前の午後8時30分に閉まります。「8:00 p.m.」は平日の旧閉館時刻に近い時刻ですが、新しい閉館時刻から計算すると誤りです。「9:00 p.m.」は図書館の閉館時刻であり、学習室の閉室時刻とは食い違います。「9:30 p.m.」は閉館時刻より後になるため誤りです。

本文の根拠: “Please note that the study rooms on the second floor will close 30 minutes before the library closes.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 本文の点検：本文 119 語・英文に日本語がない
- ○ 本文の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 本文に誤りは見つからなかった
- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 126 語）
- ○ 本文なしの確認（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：本文を見せずに解かせた答えは X（本文がないと分からない）
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（3 つの確認）。誤答の種類を付け直した
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「8:30 p.m.」は裏付けあり（引用: "Please note that the study rooms on the second floor will close 30 minutes before the library c）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない

cross

### ヒント

### 解説

訳: このにぎやかな街で道を横切る時は、注意してください。

「道を横切る」という動作を表す文脈から、この動詞が入ります。道路などの境界線を渡るという意味で使われています。

答え: cross

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 例文の点検：例文に「cross」がちょうど 1 回、ほかの選択肢の語は入っていない、訳・解説は日本語

- ○ 検証（gemma4:e4b-it-qat）：gemma4:e4b-it-qat: 条件に合う語は cross だけ

## 問題 81（#1142）英語／読解／お知らせ・広告・メール

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:52／管理番号：#1142
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Language Exchange Café: English and Japanese Night

Do you want to practice English in a relaxed atmosphere? Join us for the Language Exchange Café at Maple Community Center on Saturday, March 14. Japanese and English speakers will chat together in small groups, so everyone can take turns helping each other.

When: 6:30 p.m. to 8:30 p.m.
Where: Room 2B, Maple Community Center, 5-12 Hillside Road, Riverton
Cost: 500 yen (includes one drink)

Participants should bring a notebook and a pen. Beginners are welcome, but please come with at least a few simple English sentences in mind. Staff will divide people into groups by ability.

To reserve a seat, send your name and age to info@example.org by March 10. Seats are limited to 30 people, so early registration is strongly recommended. More information is available at www.example.org/events.

### 問題文

What must people send, and by when, to reserve a seat?

### 選択肢

A. Their phone number, no later than March 10 （誤答：本文に書かれていない／見直しでは「本文と食い違う」）
B. Their name and age, no later than March 10 （正解）
C. Their name and age, after the event has ended （誤答：本文と食い違う）
D. Their name and age, no later than March 14 （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え／見直しでは「一部だけ合っている」）

### 正解

Their name and age, no later than March 10

### ヒント

席の予約方法と締め切りは、本文の最後の段落に書かれています。

### 解説

正解は「名前と年齢を、3月10日までに送ること」です。本文には "send your name and age to info@example.org by March 10"（名前と年齢をinfo@example.orgへ3月10日までに送ってください）とあります。「締め切りが3月14日」は、イベントの日付と取り違えた誤りです。「電話番号」は本文に書かれていません。「イベントの後に送る」は、締め切りの3月10日より後になるので、本文と食い違います。

本文の根拠: “To reserve a seat, send your name and age to info@example.org by March 10.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 本文の点検：本文 129 語・英文に日本語がない
- ○ 本文の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 本文に誤りは見つからなかった
- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 136 語）
- ○ 本文なしの確認（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：本文を見せずに解かせた答えは X（本文がないと分からない）
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（4 つの確認）。誤答の種類を付け直した
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「Their name and age, no later than March 10」は裏付けあり（引用: "To reserve a seat, send your name and age to info@example.org by March 10." — 理由: 名前と年齢を3月10日まで）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない
  - ○ 誤答の種類：判定が分かれた（そのまま）：「Their name and age, no later than March 」作成役 数・日時・人などの取り違え／見直し 一部だけ合っている
  - ○ 1 対 1 の判定：怪しい誤答 1 つは、どれも正解の方が成り立つ
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（3 つの確認）。誤答の種類を付け直した
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「Their name and age, no later than March 10」は裏付けあり（引用: "To reserve a seat, send your name and age to info@example.org by March 10." — 名前と年齢を3月10日までに送るこ）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない
  - ○ 誤答の種類：判定が分かれた（そのまま）：「Their phone number, no later than March 」作成役 本文に書かれていない／見直し 本文と食い違う、「Their name and age, no later than March 」作成役 数・日時・人などの取り違え／見直し 一部だけ合っている
- ○ 解説の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 誤りは見つからなかった
  - ○ 解説・例文：誤りは見つからなかった

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:52／管理番号：#1141
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Language Exchange Café: English and Japanese Night

Do you want to practice English in a relaxed atmosphere? Join us for the Language Exchange Café at Maple Community Center on Saturday, March 14. Japanese and English speakers will chat together in small groups, so everyone can take turns helping each other.

When: 6:30 p.m. to 8:30 p.m.
Where: Room 2B, Maple Community Center, 5-12 Hillside Road, Riverton
Cost: 500 yen (includes one drink)

Participants should bring a notebook and a pen. Beginners are welcome, but please come with at least a few simple English sentences in mind. Staff will divide people into groups by ability.

To reserve a seat, send your name and age to info@example.org by March 10. Seats are limited to 30 people, so early registration is strongly recommended. More information is available at www.example.org/events.

### 問題文

What are participants asked to bring to the event?

### 選択肢

A. Writing materials for taking notes （正解）
B. A dictionary and a map （誤答：本文に書かれていない）
C. A photo ID card and a pen （誤答：本文に書かれていない／見直しでは「一部だけ合っている」）
D. Their own drink for the café （誤答：本文と食い違う）

### 正解

Writing materials for taking notes

### ヒント

参加者の持ち物について書かれているのは、本文の第3段落です。

### 解説

正解は「メモを取るための筆記用具」です。本文には "Participants should bring a notebook and a pen."（参加者はノートとペンを持ってくるべきです）とあるので、ノートとペンを筆記用具として言い換えています。「辞書と地図」は本文に書かれていません。「写真付きの身分証とペン」の身分証も本文にはありません。「自分の飲み物」は誤りです。会費の500円には飲み物1杯が含まれると書かれているので、飲み物を持参する必要はありません。

本文の根拠: “Participants should bring a notebook and a pen.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 136 語）
- ○ 本文なしの確認（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：本文を見せずに解かせた答えは X（本文がないと分からない）
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（3 つの確認）。誤答の種類を付け直した
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「Writing materials for taking notes」は裏付けあり（引用: "Participants should bring a notebook and a pen." — notebook と pen はメモ用の筆記用具で、選択肢の「notes を取るための筆）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない

## 問題 83（#1138）英語／読解／お知らせ・広告・メール

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:51／管理番号：#1138
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：選択式
- 本文作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：本文作成 API／作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 本文

Volunteers Wanted at Maple Hill Animal Shelter

Maple Hill Animal Shelter is looking for volunteers to help care for dogs and cats this spring. No experience is required, but volunteers must be at least 16 years old.

Shifts are available on Saturdays from 9:00 a.m. to 12:00 p.m. and on Wednesdays from 4:00 p.m. to 6:30 p.m. Volunteers can help with feeding, cleaning, and walking the dogs. Training sessions will be held on Saturday, April 5, from 10:00 a.m. to 11:30 a.m. at the shelter, 218 Cedar Lane.

Volunteers must sign up in advance at www.example.org/volunteer. Participants who attend at least six shifts will receive a free certificate of service. For more information, contact info@example.org.

### 問題文

At what time will the training session on April 5 end?

### 選択肢

A. 12:00 p.m. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え）
B. 6:30 p.m. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え）
C. 11:30 a.m. （正解）
D. 10:00 a.m. （誤答：数・日時・人などの取り違え）

### 正解

11:30 a.m.

### ヒント

第2段落で、4月5日の研修に関する時間の情報を探しましょう。

### 解説

正解は「午前11時30分」です。根拠は "Training sessions will be held on Saturday, April 5, from 10:00 a.m. to 11:30 a.m. at the shelter, 218 Cedar Lane."（研修は4月5日土曜日の午前10時から午前11時30分まで、Cedar Lane 218番地のシェルターで行われる）という文です。研修は午前11時30分に終わります。
「午前10時」は研修が始まる時刻です。「正午」は土曜日のシフトが終わる時刻で、研修の終了時刻ではありません。「午後6時30分」は水曜日のシフトが終わる時刻で、これも研修とは関係がありません。

本文の根拠: “Training sessions will be held on Saturday, April 5, from 10:00 a.m. to 11:30 a.m. at the shelter, 218 Cedar Lane.”

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 本文の点検：本文 110 語・英文に日本語がない
- ○ 本文の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 本文に誤りは見つからなかった
- × 設問の点検：設問 1: 誤答「Pay a fee for the training sessions.」の種類が not_stated だが、中心の語が本文にある（本文と食い違うなら contradicts、一部だけ違うなら partial、細部の違いなら detail に直す）（直しを依頼します）
- ○ 英文の点検：根拠の文が本文にある・選択肢 4 つが重複しない・英文に日本語がない（本文 117 語）
- ○ 本文なしの確認（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：本文を見せずに解かせた答えは X（本文がないと分からない）
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（2 つの確認）
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「11:30 a.m.」は裏付けあり（引用: "Training sessions will be held on Saturday, April 5, from 10:00 a.m. to 11:30 a.m. at the shelt）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 正解は 1 つに決まる（2 つの確認）
  - ○ 正解の裏付け：正解「11:30 a.m.」は裏付けあり（引用: "Training sessions will be held on Saturday, April 5, from 10:00 a.m. to 11:30 a.m. at the shelt）
  - ○ 正解が 1 つか：ほかに裏付けのある選択肢はない

Before the long run, she always takes ten minutes to ( s _ _ _ _ _ _ ) her legs to avoid injury.

stretch

### ヒント

### 解説

訳: 長距離を走る前、彼女はけがを防ぐために、いつも十分間かけて脚を伸ばす。

「けがを防ぐために脚を伸ばす」という文脈から、筋肉や体の部分を伸ばすという意味の動詞が入ります。「to」の後に続くので、動詞の原形のまま使います。

答え: stretch

## 問題 85（#1134）英語／語彙／スペル

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:50／管理番号：#1134
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：キーボード入力
- 作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 問題文

日本語の意味に合う英単語を書け（8 文字。最初の文字は示してある）。

意味: 顔立ちの整った（形容詞）

Everyone in the class agreed that the new transfer student was quite ( h _ _ _ _ _ _ _ ).

### 正解

handsome

### ヒント

最後の文字は「e」です。2 文字目は「a」です。

### 解説

訳: クラスの全員が、新しく転校してきた生徒はとてもかっこいい（顔立ちの整った）と認めた。

「handsome」は形容詞で、人の顔立ちが整っていて見栄えがよいという意味を表します。文中では「was」の後に置かれ、転校生の外見を説明する語として入ります。

答え: handsome

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 例文の点検：例文に「handsome」がちょうど 1 回、ほかの選択肢の語は入っていない、訳・解説は日本語

## 問題 86（#1133）英語／語彙／スペル

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:50／管理番号：#1133
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：キーボード入力
- 作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2
- 思考モード：作成 API／検証 API／見直し API

### 問題文

日本語の意味に合う英単語を書け（4 文字。最初の文字は示してある）。

意味: 祈る（動詞）

Every morning, my grandmother likes to ( p _ _ _ ) for her family's health.

### 正解

pray

### ヒント

最後の文字は「y」です。

### 解説

訳: 毎朝、祖母は家族の健康を祈るのが好きです。

「家族の健康を」願う行為を表す動詞が入ります。「likes to」の後には動詞の原形が来るため、pray はそのままの形で使います。「pray for ～」で「～のために祈る」という意味になります。

答え: pray

### 検証と見直しの記録

- × 例文の点検：例文に「pray」がそのままの形で 0 回（ちょうど 1 回、語尾を変えずに使う）（直しを依頼します）
- ○ 例文の点検：例文に「pray」がちょうど 1 回、ほかの選択肢の語は入っていない、訳・解説は日本語
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 条件に合う語は pray だけ
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 条件に合う語は pray だけ
- ○ 解説の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 誤りは見つからなかった
  - ○ 例文の筋：前半と後半が自然につながる
  - ○ 解説・例文：誤りは見つからなかった

## 問題 87（#1132）英語／語彙／スペル

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:49／管理番号：#1132
- レベル：高校基礎／形式：キーボード入力
- 作成役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5／検証役：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5 ×2

### 問題文

日本語の意味に合う英単語を書け（9 文字。最初の文字は示してある）。

意味: 下稽古（名詞）

Before the school festival, our drama club held a full ( r _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ ) in the auditorium to check the timing of each scene.

### 正解

rehearsal

### ヒント

最後の文字は「l」です。2 文字目は「e」です。

### 解説

訳: 文化祭の前に、演劇部は各場面のタイミングを確認するために、講堂で通し稽古を行った。

「下稽古」は本番の前に行う練習のことです。文化祭の前に各場面のタイミングを確認するため講堂で行う練習なので、本番前の練習を表す rehearsal が入ります。

答え: rehearsal

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 例文の点検：例文に「rehearsal」がちょうど 1 回、ほかの選択肢の語は入っていない、訳・解説は日本語
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 条件に合う語は rehearsal だけ
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 条件に合う語は rehearsal だけ

  - ○ 例文の筋：前半と後半が自然につながる
  - ○ 解説・例文：誤りは見つからなかった

- 作成日時：2026/10/29 19:49／管理番号：#1131

### 問題文

日本語の意味に合う英単語を書け（5 文字。最初の文字は示してある）。

意味: 手袋（名詞）

I lost my left ( g _ _ _ _ ) on the way home, so my hand was freezing.

### 正解

glove

### ヒント

最後の文字は「e」です。

### 解説

訳: 帰り道で左手の手袋をなくしてしまったので、手がかじかむほど冷たかった。

左手の手袋をなくしたという文脈から、手にはめる「手袋」が入ります。手にはめるものを表す名詞で、ここでは片方の手袋を指すので単数形になります。

答え: glove

### 検証と見直しの記録

- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 条件に合う語は glove だけ
- ○ 検証（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 条件に合う語は glove だけ
- ○ 解説の見直し（anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5）：anthropic/claude-haiku-5-5: 誤りは見つからなかった
